They are the working bar, not aspirations.\\n\\n  LINEAR        45-90s per reel. ONE job-to-be-done per video. Dark, dense, almost\\n                no narration. The changelog reads like a director's cut: a 30s\\n                walkthrough, then the technical detail. Nothing is explained twice.\\n  STRIPE        Under 90s. The API is the HERO -- code and payment flows are the\\n                motion, not decoration around a talking head. Technical viewers see\\n                the primitive; business viewers see the outcome. Same frames.\\n  VERCEL        The homepage demo is push-code -> watch-it-deploy. Brevity IS the\\n                message: it reproduces the aha moment rather than describing it.\\n  ARCADE        Cinematic polish as a floor, not a differentiator: smooth easing,\\n                deliberate zoom, no jump cuts between unrelated states.\\n\\nTHE GOVERNING RULE, from the same body of work:\\n  Identify the product's single most impressive moment and build the ENTIRE demo\\n  around it. If a first-time viewer's jaw drops inside 30 seconds, it works.\\n\\nSo when you score, ask specifically:\\n  - WHICH single moment is this demo built around? Name it and its timestamp. If you\\n    cannot find one, storyboard_clarity is 0 regardless of how polished the rest is.\\n  - Does that moment land inside the first 30s? If it is buried at the end behind\\n    setup, say so as a P1 with the timestamp it should move to.\\n  - Is every second before it EARNING that moment, or is it product tour filler?\\n  - Would LINEAR ship this length? If it is over 90s, justify every extra second or\\n    call it a defect.\\n  - Is the motion revealing evidence (Stripe: the API is the hero) or decorating a\\n    static screenshot?\\nYou are judging a rendered product-walkthrough video\\n(a feature demo with an animated cursor, click ripples, step captions, and a progress bar —\\npossibly with narration).\\n\\nTHE AUDIENCE YOU ARE JUDGING FOR IS: a smart newcomer who has never seen this product and does not know the domain jargon\\nScore every COMPREHENSION dimension from that person's seat. You are not scoring whether the\\nproduct is good; you are scoring whether THAT PERSON would understand it. When the audience is\\nnon-technical, an unexplained term is a defect even if it is correct.\\n\\nThe quality bar is STORY-FIRST and ANTI-HERO-SHOT. Camera moves should reveal evidence, not fake\\nexcellence. A viewer must always see the empty state, where the cursor clicked, any loading\\nstate, and the result — never just a polished final state.\\n\\nScore each dimension 0-2 (0=fails, 1=acceptable, 2=strong) WITH specific evidence + timestamps.\\n\\n=== CRAFT (is it well made) ===\\n1. storyboard_clarity - can a first-time viewer state what is being compared, why it matters, and what each scene proves?\\n2. state_coverage - does each flow show empty state -> action -> (loading if async) -> result, or does it skip to outcomes (hero-shot smell)?\\n3. cursor_truth - does the cursor visibly travel to and land ON the control being used before each state change?\\n4. caption_sync - do step captions match what is actually happening on screen (and any narration heard)?\\n5. pacing - can a first-time viewer read each caption and register each state? any dead air or rushed beats?\\n6. legibility - is app text readable at the rendered size? are captions large and contrasty enough?\\n7. proof_feel - does it read as evidence of a real working product (real states, real data motion) rather than staged marketing?\\n8. safety - any visible secrets, API keys, tokens, real personal data, or internal URLs that should not ship?\\n9. signature_moment - is there ONE moment the whole demo is built around, does it land early, and is everything before it earning it? (a demo that is uniformly pleasant and has no peak scores 0)\\n10. loop_etiquette - if this loops as a GIF, is the total length and final-state hold reasonable (viewers lost on the second loop = too long)?\\n11. soundtrack - is there audio at all, and does it have SHAPE that follows the story (lifting at the reveal, thinning where something must be read) rather than a flat loop? Silence scores 0. Music that fights the content, or sits so loud the demo feels like an advert, scores 1.\\n12. audio_sync - do clicks, transitions and reveals get an audible acknowledgement landing ON the frame, not near it? A demo where nothing is heard when the pointer acts feels like a mockup even when the product is real.\\n\\n=== COMPREHENSION (did anyone understand it) — judged as a smart newcomer who has never seen this product and does not know the domain jargon ===\\n1. persona - is it visible WHO this is for? A viewer should be able to name the person whose job this is, from what is on screen — not infer it from the domain. 0 if the demo never establishes a user at all.\\n2. purpose - is the JOB TO BE DONE stated? What was hard or slow or untrustworthy before? A feature tour with no stated problem scores 0 however pretty it is.\\n3. use_case - is there a concrete, specific situation driving the demo — a real question a real person would actually have — rather than a generic sample query?\\n4. feature_legibility - can a viewer name the FEATURES they just watched, as distinct capabilities, and say what each one does? Not 'it made a chart' but 'it planned the buckets, probed each one, and declared the overlap'.\\n5. full_interaction - is the interaction shown END TO END — including the parts that are boring or slow — or does it cut from click to finished result? Every hidden step is a step the viewer assumes was faked.\\n6. responsiveness - does the product visibly RESPOND: hover states, focus, loading indicators, streaming, progressive results, latency the viewer can feel? A demo where nothing acknowledges the user reads as a mockup.\\n7. flow - do the scenes connect causally — this, THEREFORE that — so the demo is a single journey rather than a list of screens? Name where the thread breaks if it does.\\n8. result - is the OUTCOME unmistakable? What does the user now have that they did not have at the start, stated plainly and left on screen long enough to absorb?\\n9. lay_sense - AUDIENCE TEST, the one most demos fail. Score from the seat of the named audience. Would THEY finish this video able to say, in their own words, why this is useful and why it should be believed? Jargon that is never unpacked, acronyms, and unexplained numbers all cost points here.\\n10. own_case_transfer - could a viewer now use this on THEIR OWN input? Is the entry point shown (where you type, what a good question looks like, what varies vs what is fixed)? A demo that only ever proves one hardcoded example scores 0.\\n\\nThese two blocks fail INDEPENDENTLY and you must let them. A cut can be beautifully made and\\nstill leave the audience unable to say who it is for — that is a high CRAFT score next to a low\\nCOMPREHENSION score, and it is the single most common real result. Do not let a strong craft\\nimpression lift the comprehension numbers.\\n\\nANTI-UNIFORMITY, and this is enforced. Two materially different cuts of the same demo (31.9s/11\\nsteps and 47.4s/16 steps) both scored exactly 1/2 on all ten dimensions of the earlier rubric.\\nThat is not a judgement, it is a shrug, and a gate that returns the same verdict regardless of\\ninput is not a gate. So:\\n\\n  - You MUST NOT give every dimension the same score. If your first pass is uniform, you have\\n    described the video instead of judging it -- go back and force a ranking.\\n  - Name the SINGLE WEAKEST and SINGLE STRONGEST dimension across BOTH blocks, in \\\"weakest\\\" and\\n    \\\"strongest\\\". They must differ. Also name \\\"weakest_comprehension\\\" specifically, because that\\n    is the one the next cut has to fix.\\n  - A 2 means \\\"as good as the Linear/Stripe/Vercel reference for that dimension\\\". A 1 means\\n    \\\"works, but a reference cut would not ship it like this\\\". A 0 means the dimension is absent\\n    or actively misleading. Most dimensions in most demos are NOT 2s.\\n  - Evidence must be CRITICAL, not descriptive. \\\"Cursor lands on UI controls\\\" is a description\\n    and scores nothing. \\\"Cursor arrives 4 frames before the click with no deceleration, so the\\n    landing reads as a teleport\\\" is evidence.\\n\\nThen list DEFECTS: each with timestamp, severity (P0 blocks publishing / P1 fix before posting /\\nP2 polish, log and ship), what you observed, and a concrete fix.\\n\\nThen, in \\\"next_cut\\\", give the REVISION BRIEF: 2-5 concrete storyboard changes that would raise\\nthe weakest comprehension dimensions, each as {\\\"dimension\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"change\\\":\\\"add/replace/extend a\\nstep, described precisely enough to implement\\\",\\\"where\\\":\\\"m:ss or 'opening'/'closing'\\\"}. This field\\nis the point of the whole judgement — a score with no actionable next cut is a grade, not a gate.\\n\\nFinally an overall verdict: publish | fix-then-publish | rework.\\n\\nReturn STRICT JSON:\\n{\\\"audience\\\":\\\"a smart newcomer who has never seen this product and does not know the domain jargon\\\",\\n \\\"scores\\\":{\\\"<dimension>\\\":{\\\"score\\\":n,\\\"evidence\\\":\\\"...\\\"}, ...all 22 dimensions...},\\n \\\"signature_moment_ts\\\":\\\"m:ss\\\",\\\"weakest\\\":\\\"<dimension>\\\",\\\"strongest\\\":\\\"<dimension>\\\",\\n \\\"weakest_comprehension\\\":\\\"<dimension>\\\",\\n \\\"defects\\\":[{\\\"ts\\\":\\\"m:ss\\\",\\\"severity\\\":\\\"P0|P1|P2\\\",\\\"observed\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"fix\\\":\\\"...\\\"}],\\n \\\"next_cut\\\":[{\\\"dimension\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"change\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"where\\\":\\\"...\\\"}],\\n \\\"verdict\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"summary\\\":\\\"2-3 sentences\\\"}\"}]}],\"generationConfig\":{\"temperature\":0.2,\"response_mime_type\":\"application/json\"}}"

A demo where nothing acknowledges the user reads as a mockup.\\n7. flow - do the scenes connect causally — this, THEREFORE that — so the demo is a single journey rather than a list of screens? Name where the thread breaks if it does.\\n8. result - is the OUTCOME unmistakable? What does the user now have that they did not have at the start, stated plainly and left on screen long enough to absorb?\\n9. lay_sense - AUDIENCE TEST, the one most demos fail. Score from the seat of the named audience. Would THEY finish this video able to say, in their own words, why this is useful and why it should be believed? Jargon that is never unpacked, acronyms, and unexplained numbers all cost points here.\\n10. own_case_transfer - could a viewer now use this on THEIR OWN input? Is the entry point shown (where you type, what a good question looks like, what varies vs what is fixed)? A demo that only ever proves one hardcoded example scores 0.\\n\\nThese two blocks fail INDEPENDENTLY and you must let them. A cut can be beautifully made and\\nstill leave the audience unable to say who it is for — that is a high CRAFT score next to a low\\nCOMPREHENSION score, and it is the single most common real result. Do not let a strong craft\\nimpression lift the comprehension numbers.\\n\\nANTI-UNIFORMITY, and this is enforced. Two materially different cuts of the same demo (31.9s/11\\nsteps and 47.4s/16 steps) both scored exactly 1/2 on all ten dimensions of the earlier rubric.\\nThat is not a judgement, it is a shrug, and a gate that returns the same verdict regardless of\\ninput is not a gate. So:\\n\\n  - You MUST NOT give every dimension the same score. If your first pass is uniform, you have\\n    described the video instead of judging it -- go back and force a ranking.\\n  - Name the SINGLE WEAKEST and SINGLE STRONGEST dimension across BOTH blocks, in \\\"weakest\\\" and\\n    \\\"strongest\\\". They must differ. Also name \\\"weakest_comprehension\\\" specifically, because that\\n    is the one the next cut has to fix.\\n  - A 2 means \\\"as good as the Linear/Stripe/Vercel reference for that dimension\\\". A 1 means\\n    \\\"works, but a reference cut would not ship it like this\\\". A 0 means the dimension is absent\\n    or actively misleading. Most dimensions in most demos are NOT 2s.\\n  - Evidence must be CRITICAL, not descriptive. \\\"Cursor lands on UI controls\\\" is a description\\n    and scores nothing. \\\"Cursor arrives 4 frames before the click with no deceleration, so the\\n    landing reads as a teleport\\\" is evidence.\\n\\nThen list DEFECTS: each with timestamp, severity (P0 blocks publishing / P1 fix before posting /\\nP2 polish, log and ship), what you observed, and a concrete fix.\\n\\nThen, in \\\"next_cut\\\", give the REVISION BRIEF: 2-5 concrete storyboard changes that would raise\\nthe weakest comprehension dimensions, each as {\\\"dimension\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"change\\\":\\\"add/replace/extend a\\nstep, described precisely enough to implement\\\",\\\"where\\\":\\\"m:ss or 'opening'/'closing'\\\"}. This field\\nis the point of the whole judgement — a score with no actionable next cut is a grade, not a gate.\\n\\nFinally an overall verdict: publish | fix-then-publish | rework.\\n\\nReturn STRICT JSON:\\n{\\\"audience\\\":\\\"a smart newcomer who has never seen this product and does not know the domain jargon\\\",\\n \\\"scores\\\":{\\\"<dimension>\\\":{\\\"score\\\":n,\\\"evidence\\\":\\\"...\\\"}, ...all 22 dimensions...},\\n \\\"signature_moment_ts\\\":\\\"m:ss\\\",\\\"weakest\\\":\\\"<dimension>\\\",\\\"strongest\\\":\\\"<dimension>\\\",\\n \\\"weakest_comprehension\\\":\\\"<dimension>\\\",\\n \\\"defects\\\":[{\\\"ts\\\":\\\"m:ss\\\",\\\"severity\\\":\\\"P0|P1|P2\\\",\\\"observed\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"fix\\\":\\\"...\\\"}],\\n \\\"next_cut\\\":[{\\\"dimension\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"change\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"where\\\":\\\"...\\\"}],\\n \\\"verdict\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"summary\\\":\\\"2-3 sentences\\\"}\"}]}],\"generationConfig\":{\"temperature\":0.2,\"response_mime_type\":\"application/json\"}}"

(a demo that is uniformly pleasant and has no peak scores 0)\\n10. loop_etiquette - if this loops as a GIF, is the total length and final-state hold reasonable (viewers lost on the second loop = too long)?\\n11. soundtrack - is there audio at all, and does it have SHAPE that follows the story (lifting at the reveal, thinning where something must be read) rather than a flat loop? If you\\n    cannot find one, storyboard_clarity is 0 regardless of how polished the rest is.\\n  - Does that moment land inside the first 30s? Camera moves should reveal evidence, not fake\\nexcellence. A viewer must always see the empty state, where the cursor clicked, any loading\\nstate, and the result — never just a polished final state.\\n\\nScore each dimension 0-2 (0=fails, 1=acceptable, 2=strong) WITH specific evidence + timestamps.\\n\\n=== CRAFT (is it well made) ===\\n1. storyboard_clarity - can a first-time viewer state what is being compared, why it matters, and what each scene proves?\\n2. state_coverage - does each flow show empty state -> action -> (loading if async) -> result, or does it skip to outcomes (hero-shot smell)?\\n3. cursor_truth - does the cursor visibly travel to and land ON the control being used before each state change?\\n4. caption_sync - do step captions match what is actually happening on screen (and any narration heard)?\\n5. pacing - can a first-time viewer read each caption and register each state? any dead air or rushed beats?\\n6. legibility - is app text readable at the rendered size? are captions large and contrasty enough?\\n7. proof_feel - does it read as evidence of a real working product (real states, real data motion) rather than staged marketing?\\n8. safety - any visible secrets, API keys, tokens, real personal data, or internal URLs that should not ship?\\n9. signature_moment - is there ONE moment the whole demo is built around, does it land early, and is everything before it earning it? (a demo that is uniformly pleasant and has no peak scores 0)\\n10. loop_etiquette - if this loops as a GIF, is the total length and final-state hold reasonable (viewers lost on the second loop = too long)?\\n11. soundtrack - is there audio at all, and does it have SHAPE that follows the story (lifting at the reveal, thinning where something must be read) rather than a flat loop? Silence scores 0. Music that fights the content, or sits so loud the demo feels like an advert, scores 1.\\n12. audio_sync - do clicks, transitions and reveals get an audible acknowledgement landing ON the frame, not near it? A demo where nothing is heard when the pointer acts feels like a mockup even when the product is real.\\n\\n=== COMPREHENSION (did anyone understand it) — judged as a smart newcomer who has never seen this product and does not know the domain jargon ===\\n1. persona - is it visible WHO this is for? A viewer should be able to name the person whose job this is, from what is on screen — not infer it from the domain. 0 if the demo never establishes a user at all.\\n2. purpose - is the JOB TO BE DONE stated? What was hard or slow or untrustworthy before? A feature tour with no stated problem scores 0 however pretty it is.\\n3. use_case - is there a concrete, specific situation driving the demo — a real question a real person would actually have — rather than a generic sample query?\\n4. feature_legibility - can a viewer name the FEATURES they just watched, as distinct capabilities, and say what each one does? Not 'it made a chart' but 'it planned the buckets, probed each one, and declared the overlap'.\\n5. full_interaction - is the interaction shown END TO END — including the parts that are boring or slow — or does it cut from click to finished result? Every hidden step is a step the viewer assumes was faked.\\n6. responsiveness - does the product visibly RESPOND: hover states, focus, loading indicators, streaming, progressive results, latency the viewer can feel? A demo where nothing acknowledges the user reads as a mockup.\\n7. flow - do the scenes connect causally — this, THEREFORE that — so the demo is a single journey rather than a list of screens? Name where the thread breaks if it does.\\n8. result - is the OUTCOME unmistakable? What does the user now have that they did not have at the start, stated plainly and left on screen long enough to absorb?\\n9. lay_sense - AUDIENCE TEST, the one most demos fail. Score from the seat of the named audience. Would THEY finish this video able to say, in their own words, why this is useful and why it should be believed? Jargon that is never unpacked, acronyms, and unexplained numbers all cost points here.\\n10. own_case_transfer - could a viewer now use this on THEIR OWN input? Is the entry point shown (where you type, what a good question looks like, what varies vs what is fixed)?               "body": "{\"contents\":[{\"parts\":[{\"inline_data\":{\"mime_type\":\"video/mp4\",\"data\":\"c3ludGhldGljIG5vbi1tZWRpYSBieXRlcw==\"}},{\"text\":\"\\nCALIBRATE AGAINST THESE. They are the working bar, not aspirations.\\n\\n  LINEAR        45-90s per reel. ONE job-to-be-done per video. Dark, dense, almost\\n                no narration. The changelog reads like a director's cut: a 30s\\n                walkthrough, then the technical detail. Nothing is explained twice.\\n  STRIPE        Under 90s. The API is the HERO -- code and payment flows are the\\n                motion, not decoration around a talking head. Technical viewers see\\n                the primitive; business viewers see the outcome. Same frames.\\n  VERCEL        The homepage demo is push-code -> watch-it-deploy. Brevity IS the\\n                message: it reproduces the aha moment rather than describing it.\\n  ARCADE        Cinematic polish as a floor, not a differentiator: smooth easing,\\n                deliberate zoom, no jump cuts between unrelated states.\\n\\nTHE GOVERNING RULE, from the same body of work:\\n  Identify the product's single most impressive moment and build the ENTIRE demo\\n  around it. If a first-time viewer's jaw drops inside 30 seconds, it works.\\n\\nSo when you score, ask specifically:\\n  - WHICH single moment is this demo built around? Name it and its timestamp. If you\\n    cannot find one, storyboard_clarity is 0 regardless of how polished the rest is.\\n  - Does that moment land inside the first 30s? If it is buried at the end behind\\n    setup, say so as a P1 with the timestamp it should move to.\\n  - Is every second before it EARNING that moment, or is it product tour filler?\\n  - Would LINEAR ship this length? If it is over 90s, justify every extra second or\\n    call it a defect.\\n  - Is the motion revealing evidence (Stripe: the API is the hero) or decorating a\\n    static screenshot?\\nYou are judging a rendered product-walkthrough video\\n(a feature demo with an animated cursor, click ripples, step captions, and a progress bar —\\npossibly with narration).\\n\\nTHE AUDIENCE YOU ARE JUDGING FOR IS: a smart newcomer who has never seen this product and does not know the domain jargon\\nScore every COMPREHENSION dimension from that person's seat. You are not scoring whether the\\nproduct is good; you are scoring whether THAT PERSON would understand it. When the audience is\\nnon-technical, an unexplained term is a defect even if it is correct.\\n\\nThe quality bar is STORY-FIRST and ANTI-HERO-SHOT. Camera moves should reveal evidence, not fake\\nexcellence. A viewer must always see the empty state, where the cursor clicked, any loading\\nstate, and the result — never just a polished final state.\\n\\nScore each dimension 0-2 (0=fails, 1=acceptable, 2=strong) WITH specific evidence + timestamps.\\n\\n=== CRAFT (is it well made) ===\\n1. storyboard_clarity - can a first-time viewer state what is being compared, why it matters, and what each scene proves?\\n2. state_coverage - does each flow show empty state -> action -> (loading if async) -> result, or does it skip to outcomes (hero-shot smell)?\\n3. cursor_truth - does the cursor visibly travel to and land ON the control being used before each state change?\\n4. caption_sync - do step captions match what is actually happening on screen (and any narration heard)?\\n5. pacing - can a first-time viewer read each caption and register each state? any dead air or rushed beats?\\n6. legibility - is app text readable at the rendered size? are captions large and contrasty enough?\\n7. proof_feel - does it read as evidence of a real working product (real states, real data motion) rather than staged marketing?\\n8. safety - any visible secrets, API keys, tokens, real personal data, or internal URLs that should not ship?\\n9. signature_moment - is there ONE moment the whole demo is built around, does it land early, and is everything before it earning it? (a demo that is uniformly pleasant and has no peak scores 0)\\n10. loop_etiquette - if this loops as a GIF, is the total length and final-state hold reasonable (viewers lost on the second loop = too long)?\\n11. soundtrack - is there audio at all, and does it have SHAPE that follows the story (lifting at the reveal, thinning where something must be read) rather than a flat loop? Silence scores 0. Music that fights the content, or sits so loud the demo feels like an advert, scores 1.\\n12. audio_sync - do clicks, transitions and reveals get an audible acknowledgement landing ON the frame, not near it? A demo where nothing is heard when the pointer acts feels like a mockup even when the product is real.\\n\\n=== COMPREHENSION (did anyone understand it) — judged as a smart newcomer who has never seen this product and does not know the domain jargon ===\\n1. persona - is it visible WHO this is for? A viewer should be able to name the person whose job this is, from what is on screen — not infer it from the domain. 0 if the demo never establishes a user at all.\\n2. purpose - is the JOB TO BE DONE stated? What was hard or slow or untrustworthy before? A feature tour with no stated problem scores 0 however pretty it is.\\n3. use_case - is there a concrete, specific situation driving the demo — a real question a real person would actually have — rather than a generic sample query?\\n4. feature_legibility - can a viewer name the FEATURES they just watched, as distinct capabilities, and say what each one does? Not 'it made a chart' but 'it planned the buckets, probed each one, and declared the overlap'.\\n5. full_interaction - is the interaction shown END TO END — including the parts that are boring or slow — or does it cut from click to finished result? Every hidden step is a step the viewer assumes was faked.\\n6. responsiveness - does the product visibly RESPOND: hover states, focus, loading indicators, streaming, progressive results, latency the viewer can feel? A demo where nothing acknowledges the user reads as a mockup.\\n7. flow - do the scenes connect causally — this, THEREFORE that — so the demo is a single journey rather than a list of screens? Name where the thread breaks if it does.\\n8. result - is the OUTCOME unmistakable? What does the user now have that they did not have at the start, stated plainly and left on screen long enough to absorb?\\n9. lay_sense - AUDIENCE TEST, the one most demos fail. Score from the seat of the named audience. Would THEY finish this video able to say, in their own words, why this is useful and why it should be believed? Jargon that is never unpacked, acronyms, and unexplained numbers all cost points here.\\n10. own_case_transfer - could a viewer now use this on THEIR OWN input? Is the entry point shown (where you type, what a good question looks like, what varies vs what is fixed)? A demo that only ever proves one hardcoded example scores 0.\\n\\nThese two blocks fail INDEPENDENTLY and you must let them. A cut can be beautifully made and\\nstill leave the audience unable to say who it is for — that is a high CRAFT score next to a low\\nCOMPREHENSION score, and it is the single most common real result. Do not let a strong craft\\nimpression lift the comprehension numbers.\\n\\nANTI-UNIFORMITY, and this is enforced. Two materially different cuts of the same demo (31.9s/11\\nsteps and 47.4s/16 steps) both scored exactly 1/2 on all ten dimensions of the earlier rubric.\\nThat is not a judgement, it is a shrug, and a gate that returns the same verdict regardless of\\ninput is not a gate. So:\\n\\n  - You MUST NOT give every dimension the same score. If your first pass is uniform, you have\\n    described the video instead of judging it -- go back and force a ranking.\\n  - Name the SINGLE WEAKEST and SINGLE STRONGEST dimension across BOTH blocks, in \\\"weakest\\\" and\\n    \\\"strongest\\\". They must differ. Also name \\\"weakest_comprehension\\\" specifically, because that\\n    is the one the next cut has to fix.\\n  - A 2 means \\\"as good as the Linear/Stripe/Vercel reference for that dimension\\\". A 1 means\\n    \\\"works, but a reference cut would not ship it like this\\\". A 0 means the dimension is absent\\n    or actively misleading. Most dimensions in most demos are NOT 2s.\\n  - Evidence must be CRITICAL, not descriptive. \\\"Cursor lands on UI controls\\\" is a description\\n    and scores nothing. \\\"Cursor arrives 4 frames before the click with no deceleration, so the\\n    landing reads as a teleport\\\" is evidence.\\n\\nThen list DEFECTS: each with timestamp, severity (P0 blocks publishing / P1 fix before posting /\\nP2 polish, log and ship), what you observed, and a concrete fix.\\n\\nThen, in \\\"next_cut\\\", give the REVISION BRIEF: 2-5 concrete storyboard changes that would raise\\nthe weakest comprehension dimensions, each as {\\\"dimension\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"change\\\":\\\"add/replace/extend a\\nstep, described precisely enough to implement\\\",\\\"where\\\":\\\"m:ss or 'opening'/'closing'\\\"}. This field\\nis the point of the whole judgement — a score with no actionable next cut is a grade, not a gate.\\n\\nFinally an overall verdict: publish | fix-then-publish | rework.\\n\\nReturn STRICT JSON:\\n{\\\"audience\\\":\\\"a smart newcomer who has never seen this product and does not know the domain jargon\\\",\\n \\\"scores\\\":{\\\"<dimension>\\\":{\\\"score\\\":n,\\\"evidence\\\":\\\"...\\\"}, ...all 22 dimensions...},\\n \\\"signature_moment_ts\\\":\\\"m:ss\\\",\\\"weakest\\\":\\\"<dimension>\\\",\\\"strongest\\\":\\\"<dimension>\\\",\\n \\\"weakest_comprehension\\\":\\\"<dimension>\\\",\\n \\\"defects\\\":[{\\\"ts\\\":\\\"m:ss\\\",\\\"severity\\\":\\\"P0|P1|P2\\\",\\\"observed\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"fix\\\":\\\"...\\\"}],\\n \\\"next_cut\\\":[{\\\"dimension\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"change\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"where\\\":\\\"...\\\"}],\\n \\\"verdict\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"summary\\\":\\\"2-3 sentences\\\"}\"}]}],\"generationConfig\":{\"temperature\":0.2,\"response_mime_type\":\"application/json\"}}"

(a demo that is uniformly pleasant and has no peak scores 0)\\n10. loop_etiquette - if this loops as a GIF, is the total length and final-state hold reasonable (viewers lost on the second loop = too long)?\\n11. soundtrack - is there audio at all, and does it have SHAPE that follows the story (lifting at the reveal, thinning where something must be read) rather than a flat loop? Silence scores 0. Music that fights the content, or sits so loud the demo feels like an advert, scores 1.\\n12. audio_sync - do clicks, transitions and reveals get an audible acknowledgement landing ON the frame, not near it? A demo where nothing is heard when the pointer acts feels like a mockup even when the product is real.\\n\\n=== COMPREHENSION (did anyone understand it) — judged as a smart newcomer who has never seen this product and does not know the domain jargon ===\\n1. persona - is it visible WHO this is for? A viewer should be able to name the person whose job this is, from what is on screen — not infer it from the domain. 0 if the demo never establishes a user at all.\\n2. purpose - is the JOB TO BE DONE stated? What was hard or slow or untrustworthy before? A feature tour with no stated problem scores 0 however pretty it is.\\n3. use_case - is there a concrete, specific situation driving the demo — a real question a real person would actually have — rather than a generic sample query?\\n4. feature_legibility - can a viewer name the FEATURES they just watched, as distinct capabilities, and say what each one does? Not 'it made a chart' but 'it planned the buckets, probed each one, and declared the overlap'.\\n5. full_interaction - is the interaction shown END TO END — including the parts that are boring or slow — or does it cut from click to finished result? Every hidden step is a step the viewer assumes was faked.\\n6. responsiveness - does the product visibly RESPOND: hover states, focus, loading indicators, streaming, progressive results, latency the viewer can feel? A demo where nothing acknowledges the user reads as a mockup.\\n7. flow - do the scenes connect causally — this, THEREFORE that — so the demo is a single journey rather than a list of screens? Name where the thread breaks if it does.\\n8. result - is the OUTCOME unmistakable? What does the user now have that they did not have at the start, stated plainly and left on screen long enough to absorb?\\n9. lay_sense - AUDIENCE TEST, the one most demos fail. Score from the seat of the named audience. Would THEY finish this video able to say, in their own words, why this is useful and why it should be believed? Jargon that is never unpacked, acronyms, and unexplained numbers all cost points here.\\n10. own_case_transfer - could a viewer now use this on THEIR OWN input? Is the entry point shown (where you type, what a good question looks like, what varies vs what is fixed)? A demo that only ever proves one hardcoded example scores 0.\\n\\nThese two blocks fail INDEPENDENTLY and you must let them. A cut can be beautifully made and\\nstill leave the audience unable to say who it is for — that is a high CRAFT score next to a low\\nCOMPREHENSION score, and it is the single most common real result. Do not let a strong craft\\nimpression lift the comprehension numbers.\\n\\nANTI-UNIFORMITY, and this is enforced. Two materially different cuts of the same demo (31.9s/11\\nsteps and 47.4s/16 steps) both scored exactly 1/2 on all ten dimensions of the earlier rubric.\\nThat is not a judgement, it is a shrug, and a gate that returns the same verdict regardless of\\ninput is not a gate. So:\\n\\n  - You MUST NOT give every dimension the same score. If your first pass is uniform, you have\\n    described the video instead of judging it -- go back and force a ranking.\\n  - Name the SINGLE WEAKEST and SINGLE STRONGEST dimension across BOTH blocks, in \\\"weakest\\\" and\\n    \\\"strongest\\\". They must differ. Also name \\\"weakest_comprehension\\\" specifically, because that\\n    is the one the next cut has to fix.\\n  - A 2 means \\\"as good as the Linear/Stripe/Vercel reference for that dimension\\\". A 1 means\\n    \\\"works, but a reference cut would not ship it like this\\\". A 0 means the dimension is absent\\n    or actively misleading. Most dimensions in most demos are NOT 2s.\\n  - Evidence must be CRITICAL, not descriptive. \\\"Cursor lands on UI controls\\\" is a description\\n    and scores nothing. \\\"Cursor arrives 4 frames before the click with no deceleration, so the\\n    landing reads as a teleport\\\" is evidence.\\n\\nThen list DEFECTS: each with timestamp, severity (P0 blocks publishing / P1 fix before posting /\\nP2 polish, log and ship), what you observed, and a concrete fix.\\n\\nThen, in \\\"next_cut\\\", give the REVISION BRIEF: 2-5 concrete storyboard changes that would raise\\nthe weakest comprehension dimensions, each as {\\\"dimension\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"change\\\":\\\"add/replace/extend a\\nstep, described precisely enough to implement\\\",\\\"where\\\":\\\"m:ss or 'opening'/'closing'\\\"}. This field\\nis the point of the whole judgement — a score with no actionable next cut is a grade, not a gate.\\n\\nFinally an overall verdict: publish | fix-then-publish | rework.\\n\\nReturn STRICT JSON:\\n{\\\"audience\\\":\\\"a smart newcomer who has never seen this product and does not know the domain jargon\\\",\\n \\\"scores\\\":{\\\"<dimension>\\\":{\\\"score\\\":n,\\\"evidence\\\":\\\"...\\\"}, ...all 22 dimensions...},\\n \\\"signature_moment_ts\\\":\\\"m:ss\\\",\\\"weakest\\\":\\\"<dimension>\\\",\\\"strongest\\\":\\\"<dimension>\\\",\\n \\\"weakest_comprehension\\\":\\\"<dimension>\\\",\\n \\\"defects\\\":[{\\\"ts\\\":\\\"m:ss\\\",\\\"severity\\\":\\\"P0|P1|P2\\\",\\\"observed\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"fix\\\":\\\"...\\\"}],\\n \\\"next_cut\\\":[{\\\"dimension\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"change\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"where\\\":\\\"...\\\"}],\\n \\\"verdict\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"summary\\\":\\\"2-3 sentences\\\"}\"}]}],\"generationConfig\":{\"temperature\":0.2,\"response_mime_type\":\"application/json\"}}"

Not 'it made a chart' but 'it planned the buckets, probed each one, and declared the overlap'.\\n5. full_interaction - is the interaction shown END TO END — including the parts that are boring or slow — or does it cut from click to finished result? Every hidden step is a step the viewer assumes was faked.\\n6. responsiveness - does the product visibly RESPOND: hover states, focus, loading indicators, streaming, progressive results, latency the viewer can feel? A demo where nothing acknowledges the user reads as a mockup.\\n7. flow - do the scenes connect causally — this, THEREFORE that — so the demo is a single journey rather than a list of screens? Name where the thread breaks if it does.\\n8. result - is the OUTCOME unmistakable? What does the user now have that they did not have at the start, stated plainly and left on screen long enough to absorb?\\n9. lay_sense - AUDIENCE TEST, the one most demos fail. Score from the seat of the named audience. Would THEY finish this video able to say, in their own words, why this is useful and why it should be believed? Jargon that is never unpacked, acronyms, and unexplained numbers all cost points here.\\n10. own_case_transfer - could a viewer now use this on THEIR OWN input? Is the entry point shown (where you type, what a good question looks like, what varies vs what is fixed)? A demo that only ever proves one hardcoded example scores 0.\\n\\nThese two blocks fail INDEPENDENTLY and you must let them. A cut can be beautifully made and\\nstill leave the audience unable to say who it is for — that is a high CRAFT score next to a low\\nCOMPREHENSION score, and it is the single most common real result. Do not let a strong craft\\nimpression lift the comprehension numbers.\\n\\nANTI-UNIFORMITY, and this is enforced. Two materially different cuts of the same demo (31.9s/11\\nsteps and 47.4s/16 steps) both scored exactly 1/2 on all ten dimensions of the earlier rubric.\\nThat is not a judgement, it is a shrug, and a gate that returns the same verdict regardless of\\ninput is not a gate. So:\\n\\n  - You MUST NOT give every dimension the same score. If your first pass is uniform, you have\\n    described the video instead of judging it -- go back and force a ranking.\\n  - Name the SINGLE WEAKEST and SINGLE STRONGEST dimension across BOTH blocks, in \\\"weakest\\\" and\\n    \\\"strongest\\\". They must differ. Also name \\\"weakest_comprehension\\\" specifically, because that\\n    is the one the next cut has to fix.\\n  - A 2 means \\\"as good as the Linear/Stripe/Vercel reference for that dimension\\\". A 1 means\\n    \\\"works, but a reference cut would not ship it like this\\\". A 0 means the dimension is absent\\n    or actively misleading. Most dimensions in most demos are NOT 2s.\\n  - Evidence must be CRITICAL, not descriptive. \\\"Cursor lands on UI controls\\\" is a description\\n    and scores nothing. \\\"Cursor arrives 4 frames before the click with no deceleration, so the\\n    landing reads as a teleport\\\" is evidence.\\n\\nThen list DEFECTS: each with timestamp, severity (P0 blocks publishing / P1 fix before posting /\\nP2 polish, log and ship), what you observed, and a concrete fix.\\n\\nThen, in \\\"next_cut\\\", give the REVISION BRIEF: 2-5 concrete storyboard changes that would raise\\nthe weakest comprehension dimensions, each as {\\\"dimension\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"change\\\":\\\"add/replace/extend a\\nstep, described precisely enough to implement\\\",\\\"where\\\":\\\"m:ss or 'opening'/'closing'\\\"}. This field\\nis the point of the whole judgement — a score with no actionable next cut is a grade, not a gate.\\n\\nFinally an overall verdict: publish | fix-then-publish | rework.\\n\\nReturn STRICT JSON:\\n{\\\"audience\\\":\\\"a smart newcomer who has never seen this product and does not know the domain jargon\\\",\\n \\\"scores\\\":{\\\"<dimension>\\\":{\\\"score\\\":n,\\\"evidence\\\":\\\"...\\\"}, ...all 22 dimensions...},\\n \\\"signature_moment_ts\\\":\\\"m:ss\\\",\\\"weakest\\\":\\\"<dimension>\\\",\\\"strongest\\\":\\\"<dimension>\\\",\\n \\\"weakest_comprehension\\\":\\\"<dimension>\\\",\\n \\\"defects\\\":[{\\\"ts\\\":\\\"m:ss\\\",\\\"severity\\\":\\\"P0|P1|P2\\\",\\\"observed\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"fix\\\":\\\"...\\\"}],\\n \\\"next_cut\\\":[{\\\"dimension\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"change\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"where\\\":\\\"...\\\"}],\\n \\\"verdict\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"summary\\\":\\\"2-3 sentences\\\"}\"}]}],\"generationConfig\":{\"temperature\":0.2,\"response_mime_type\":\"application/json\"}}"

They are the working bar, not aspirations.\\n\\n  LINEAR        45-90s per reel. ONE job-to-be-done per video. Dark, dense, almost\\n                no narration. The changelog reads like a director's cut: a 30s\\n                walkthrough, then the technical detail. Nothing is explained twice.\\n  STRIPE        Under 90s. The API is the HERO -- code and payment flows are the\\n                motion, not decoration around a talking head. Technical viewers see\\n                the primitive; business viewers see the outcome. Same frames.\\n  VERCEL        The homepage demo is push-code -> watch-it-deploy. Brevity IS the\\n                message: it reproduces the aha moment rather than describing it.\\n  ARCADE        Cinematic polish as a floor, not a differentiator: smooth easing,\\n                deliberate zoom, no jump cuts between unrelated states.\\n\\nTHE GOVERNING RULE, from the same body of work:\\n  Identify the product's single most impressive moment and build the ENTIRE demo\\n  around it. If a first-time viewer's jaw drops inside 30 seconds, it works.\\n\\nSo when you score, ask specifically:\\n  - WHICH single moment is this demo built around? Name it and its timestamp. If you\\n    cannot find one, storyboard_clarity is 0 regardless of how polished the rest is.\\n  - Does that moment land inside the first 30s? If it is buried at the end behind\\n    setup, say so as a P1 with the timestamp it should move to.\\n  - Is every second before it EARNING that moment, or is it product tour filler?\\n  - Would LINEAR ship this length? If it is over 90s, justify every extra second or\\n    call it a defect.\\n  - Is the motion revealing evidence (Stripe: the API is the hero) or decorating a\\n    static screenshot?\\nYou are judging a rendered product-walkthrough video\\n(a feature demo with an animated cursor, click ripples, step captions, and a progress bar —\\npossibly with narration).\\n\\nTHE AUDIENCE YOU ARE JUDGING FOR IS: a smart newcomer who has never seen this product and does not know the domain jargon\\nScore every COMPREHENSION dimension from that person's seat. You are not scoring whether the\\nproduct is good; you are scoring whether THAT PERSON would understand it. When the audience is\\nnon-technical, an unexplained term is a defect even if it is correct.\\n\\nThe quality bar is STORY-FIRST and ANTI-HERO-SHOT. Camera moves should reveal evidence, not fake\\nexcellence. A viewer must always see the empty state, where the cursor clicked, any loading\\nstate, and the result — never just a polished final state.\\n\\nScore each dimension 0-2 (0=fails, 1=acceptable, 2=strong) WITH specific evidence + timestamps.\\n\\n=== CRAFT (is it well made) ===\\n1. storyboard_clarity - can a first-time viewer state what is being compared, why it matters, and what each scene proves?\\n2. state_coverage - does each flow show empty state -> action -> (loading if async) -> result, or does it skip to outcomes (hero-shot smell)?\\n3. cursor_truth - does the cursor visibly travel to and land ON the control being used before each state change?\\n4. caption_sync - do step captions match what is actually happening on screen (and any narration heard)?\\n5. pacing - can a first-time viewer read each caption and register each state? any dead air or rushed beats?\\n6. legibility - is app text readable at the rendered size? are captions large and contrasty enough?\\n7. proof_feel - does it read as evidence of a real working product (real states, real data motion) rather than staged marketing?\\n8. safety - any visible secrets, API keys, tokens, real personal data, or internal URLs that should not ship?\\n9. signature_moment - is there ONE moment the whole demo is built around, does it land early, and is everything before it earning it? (a demo that is uniformly pleasant and has no peak scores 0)\\n10. loop_etiquette - if this loops as a GIF, is the total length and final-state hold reasonable (viewers lost on the second loop = too long)?\\n11. soundtrack - is there audio at all, and does it have SHAPE that follows the story (lifting at the reveal, thinning where something must be read) rather than a flat loop? Silence scores 0. Music that fights the content, or sits so loud the demo feels like an advert, scores 1.\\n12. audio_sync - do clicks, transitions and reveals get an audible acknowledgement landing ON the frame, not near it? A demo where nothing is heard when the pointer acts feels like a mockup even when the product is real.\\n\\n=== COMPREHENSION (did anyone understand it) — judged as a smart newcomer who has never seen this product and does not know the domain jargon ===\\n1. persona - is it visible WHO this is for? A viewer should be able to name the person whose job this is, from what is on screen — not infer it from the domain. 0 if the demo never establishes a user at all.\\n2. purpose - is the JOB TO BE DONE stated? What was hard or slow or untrustworthy before? A feature tour with no stated problem scores 0 however pretty it is.\\n3. use_case - is there a concrete, specific situation driving the demo — a real question a real person would actually have — rather than a generic sample query?\\n4. feature_legibility - can a viewer name the FEATURES they just watched, as distinct capabilities, and say what each one does? Not 'it made a chart' but 'it planned the buckets, probed each one, and declared the overlap'.\\n5. full_interaction - is the interaction shown END TO END — including the parts that are boring or slow — or does it cut from click to finished result? Every hidden step is a step the viewer assumes was faked.\\n6. responsiveness - does the product visibly RESPOND: hover states, focus, loading indicators, streaming, progressive results, latency the viewer can feel? A demo where nothing acknowledges the user reads as a mockup.\\n7. flow - do the scenes connect causally — this, THEREFORE that — so the demo is a single journey rather than a list of screens? Name where the thread breaks if it does.\\n8. result - is the OUTCOME unmistakable? What does the user now have that they did not have at the start, stated plainly and left on screen long enough to absorb?\\n9. lay_sense - AUDIENCE TEST, the one most demos fail. Score from the seat of the named audience. Would THEY finish this video able to say, in their own words, why this is useful and why it should be believed? Jargon that is never unpacked, acronyms, and unexplained numbers all cost points here.\\n10. own_case_transfer - could a viewer now use this on THEIR OWN input? Is the entry point shown (where you type, what a good question looks like, what varies vs what is fixed)? A demo that only ever proves one hardcoded example scores 0.\\n\\nThese two blocks fail INDEPENDENTLY and you must let them. A cut can be beautifully made and\\nstill leave the audience unable to say who it is for — that is a high CRAFT score next to a low\\nCOMPREHENSION score, and it is the single most common real result. Do not let a strong craft\\nimpression lift the comprehension numbers.\\n\\nANTI-UNIFORMITY, and this is enforced. Two materially different cuts of the same demo (31.9s/11\\nsteps and 47.4s/16 steps) both scored exactly 1/2 on all ten dimensions of the earlier rubric.\\nThat is not a judgement, it is a shrug, and a gate that returns the same verdict regardless of\\ninput is not a gate. So:\\n\\n  - You MUST NOT give every dimension the same score. If it is over 90s, justify every extra second or\\n    call it a defect.\\n  - Is the motion revealing evidence (Stripe: the API is the hero) or decorating a\\n    static screenshot?\\nYou are judging a rendered product-walkthrough video\\n(a feature demo with an animated cursor, click ripples, step captions, and a progress bar —\\npossibly with narration).\\n\\nTHE AUDIENCE YOU ARE JUDGING FOR IS: a smart newcomer who has never seen this product and does not know the domain jargon\\nScore every COMPREHENSION dimension from that person's seat. You are not scoring whether the\\nproduct is good; you are scoring whether THAT PERSON would understand it. When the audience is\\nnon-technical, an unexplained term is a defect even if it is correct.\\n\\nThe quality bar is STORY-FIRST and ANTI-HERO-SHOT. Camera moves should reveal evidence, not fake\\nexcellence. A viewer must always see the empty state, where the cursor clicked, any loading\\nstate, and the result — never just a polished final state.\\n\\nScore each dimension 0-2 (0=fails, 1=acceptable, 2=strong) WITH specific evidence + timestamps.\\n\\n=== CRAFT (is it well made) ===\\n1. storyboard_clarity - can a first-time viewer state what is being compared, why it matters, and what each scene proves?\\n2. state_coverage - does each flow show empty state -> action -> (loading if async) -> result, or does it skip to outcomes (hero-shot smell)?\\n3. cursor_truth - does the cursor visibly travel to and land ON the control being used before each state change?\\n4. caption_sync - do step captions match what is actually happening on screen (and any narration heard)?\\n5. pacing - can a first-time viewer read each caption and register each state? any dead air or rushed beats?\\n6. legibility - is app text readable at the rendered size? are captions large and contrasty enough?\\n7. proof_feel - does it read as evidence of a real working product (real states, real data motion) rather than staged marketing?\\n8. safety - any visible secrets, API keys, tokens, real personal data, or internal URLs that should not ship?\\n9. signature_moment - is there ONE moment the whole demo is built around, does it land early, and is everything before it earning it? (a demo that is uniformly pleasant and has no peak scores 0)\\n10. loop_etiquette - if this loops as a GIF, is the total length and final-state hold reasonable (viewers lost on the second loop = too long)?\\n11. soundtrack - is there audio at all, and does it have SHAPE that follows the story (lifting at the reveal, thinning where something must be read) rather than a flat loop? Silence scores 0. Music that fights the content, or sits so loud the demo feels like an advert, scores 1.\\n12. audio_sync - do clicks, transitions and reveals get an audible acknowledgement landing ON the frame, not near it? A demo where nothing is heard when the pointer acts feels like a mockup even when the product is real.\\n\\n=== COMPREHENSION (did anyone understand it) — judged as a smart newcomer who has never seen this product and does not know the domain jargon ===\\n1. persona - is it visible WHO this is for? A viewer should be able to name the person whose job this is, from what is on screen — not infer it from the domain. 0 if the demo never establishes a user at all.\\n2. purpose - is the JOB TO BE DONE stated? What was hard or slow or untrustworthy before? A feature tour with no stated problem scores 0 however pretty it is.\\n3. use_case - is there a concrete, specific situation driving the demo — a real question a real person would actually have — rather than a generic sample query?\\n4. feature_legibility - can a viewer name the FEATURES they just watched, as distinct capabilities, and say what each one does? Not 'it made a chart' but 'it planned the buckets, probed each one, and declared the overlap'.\\n5. full_interaction - is the interaction shown END TO END — including the parts that are boring or slow — or does it cut from click to finished result? Every hidden step is a step the viewer assumes was faked.\\n6. responsiveness - does the product visibly RESPOND: hover states, focus, loading indicators, streaming, progressive results, latency the viewer can feel? A demo where nothing acknowledges the user reads as a mockup.\\n7. flow - do the scenes connect causally — this, THEREFORE that — so the demo is a single journey rather than a list of screens? Name where the thread breaks if it does.\\n8. result - is the OUTCOME unmistakable? What does the user now have that they did not have at the start, stated plainly and left on screen long enough to absorb?\\n9. lay_sense - AUDIENCE TEST, the one most demos fail. Score from the seat of the named audience. Would THEY finish this video able to say, in their own words, why this is useful and why it should be believed? Jargon that is never unpacked, acronyms, and unexplained numbers all cost points here.\\n10. own_case_transfer - could a viewer now use this on THEIR OWN input? Is the entry point shown (where you type, what a good question looks like, what varies vs what is fixed)? A demo that only ever proves one hardcoded example scores 0.\\n\\nThese two blocks fail INDEPENDENTLY and you must let them. A cut can be beautifully made and\\nstill leave the audience unable to say who it is for — that is a high CRAFT score next to a low\\nCOMPREHENSION score, and it is the single most common real result. Do not let a strong craft\\nimpression lift the comprehension numbers.\\n\\nANTI-UNIFORMITY, and this is enforced. Two materially different cuts of the same demo (31.9s/11\\nsteps and 47.4s/16 steps) both scored exactly 1/2 on all ten dimensions of the earlier rubric.\\nThat is not a judgement, it is a shrug, and a gate that returns the same verdict regardless of\\ninput is not a gate. So:\\n\\n  - You MUST NOT give every dimension the same score. If your first pass is uniform, you have\\n    described the video instead of judging it -- go back and force a ranking.\\n  - Name the SINGLE WEAKEST and SINGLE STRONGEST dimension across BOTH blocks, in \\\"weakest\\\" and\\n    \\\"strongest\\\". They must differ. Also name \\\"weakest_comprehension\\\" specifically, because that\\n    is the one the next cut has to fix.\\n  - A 2 means \\\"as good as the Linear/Stripe/Vercel reference for that dimension\\\". A 1 means\\n    \\\"works, but a reference cut would not ship it like this\\\". A 0 means the dimension is absent\\n    or actively misleading. Most dimensions in most demos are NOT 2s.\\n  - Evidence must be CRITICAL, not descriptive. \\\"Cursor lands on UI controls\\\" is a description\\n    and scores nothing. \\\"Cursor arrives 4 frames before the click with no deceleration, so the\\n    landing reads as a teleport\\\" is evidence.\\n\\nThen list DEFECTS: each with timestamp, severity (P0 blocks publishing / P1 fix before posting /\\nP2 polish, log and ship), what you observed, and a concrete fix.\\n\\nThen, in \\\"next_cut\\\", give the REVISION BRIEF: 2-5 concrete storyboard changes that would raise\\nthe weakest comprehension dimensions, each as {\\\"dimension\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"change\\\":\\\"add/replace/extend a\\nstep, described precisely enough to implement\\\",\\\"where\\\":\\\"m:ss or 'opening'/'closing'\\\"}. This field\\nis the point of the whole judgement — a score with no actionable next cut is a grade, not a gate.\\n\\nFinally an overall verdict: publish | fix-then-publish | rework.\\n\\nReturn STRICT JSON:\\n{\\\"audience\\\":\\\"a smart newcomer who has never seen this product and does not know the domain jargon\\\",\\n \\\"scores\\\":{\\\"<dimension>\\\":{\\\"score\\\":n,\\\"evidence\\\":\\\"...\\\"}, ...all 22 dimensions...},\\n \\\"signature_moment_ts\\\":\\\"m:ss\\\",\\\"weakest\\\":\\\"<dimension>\\\",\\\"strongest\\\":\\\"<dimension>\\\",\\n \\\"weakest_comprehension\\\":\\\"<dimension>\\\",\\n \\\"defects\\\":[{\\\"ts\\\":\\\"m:ss\\\",\\\"severity\\\":\\\"P0|P1|P2\\\",\\\"observed\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"fix\\\":\\\"...\\\"}],\\n \\\"next_cut\\\":[{\\\"dimension\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"change\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"where\\\":\\\"...\\\"}],\\n \\\"verdict\\\":\\\"...\\\",\\\"summary\\\":\\\"2-3 sentences\\\"}\"}]}],\"generationConfig\":{\"temperature\":0.2,\"response_mime_type\":\"application/json\"}}"
            }
          ]
        },
